<meta>This page represents the front cover of the manuscript. No written text is present on the original surface, only decorative tooling.</meta>

->**Front Cover**<-

This manuscript is encased in a sophisticated brown leather binding, preserved with its original <term>gold-tooled<note>Also known as "gilt," this is a decorative technique where gold leaf is pressed into the leather using heated brass stamps or wheels.</note></term> ornamentation. The design is architectural in nature, featuring several concentric rectangular borders that draw the eye toward the center.

The outermost border consists of a thick gold line paired with a delicate band of repeating floral and leaf-like stamps. Inside this frame, a second rectangle is connected to the central panel by diagonal gold lines at each corner, creating a "panelled" effect common in high-quality Renaissance and Baroque bookbinding.

The central vertical rectangle is framed by a dotted gold line. At each of its interior corners, the binder has placed an ornamental <term>fleur-de-lis<note>A stylized lily symbol, often associated with royalty or used as a common decorative motif.</note></term> stamp. 

In the exact center of the cover lies an ornate gold-stamped centerpiece. This heraldic-style emblem features:
*   A stylized oval shield-like shape
*   A fleur-de-lis topper
*   Surrounding decorative scrolls

The spine on the left side shows raised bands—the ridges where the sewing supports the pages—which are also decorated with gold leaf. While the leather shows some gentle wear and aging along the edges, the gilding remains a testament to the manuscript's historical value and the craftsmanship of its creator.

<summary>The front cover of the manuscript, featuring a decorative brown leather binding with elaborate gold-tooled frames and a central heraldic motif.</summary>
<keywords>binding, gold-tooling, leather, fleur-de-lis, heraldry, book arts, gilt</keywords>

# The Power of Children in the Final Time
### On the Occasion of the Little Preachers, Or, the Praying Children in Silesia <note>The "Praying Children" (Betende Kinder) refers to a Pietist phenomenon in 1707–1708 where thousands of children in Silesia gathered in open fields to pray, sing, and preach, which many contemporaries viewed as a prophetic sign of the end of the world.</note>
### Presented from the Holy Scripture
### By Johann Wilhelm Petersen, Doctor of Holy Scripture <note>Johann Wilhelm Petersen (1649–1727) was a central figure in radical Pietism, famous for his millenarian beliefs—the idea that a thousand-year kingdom of Christ was imminent.</note>

->Frankfurt and Leipzig<-
->Published by Samuel Heil and Johann Gottfried Liebezeit / 1709.<-

<summary>This is the title page of a 1709 theological work by Johann Wilhelm Petersen, which examines the "Praying Children" movement in Silesia through a biblical and eschatological lens.</summary>
<keywords>Johann Wilhelm Petersen, Praying Children, Silesia, Pietism, end times, Little Preachers, 18th-century theology</keywords>

# High-Well-Born Lord, Lord
# MAGNUS
# von Wedderkopff,

### Highly-Trusted President of the Privy Council <note>The Privy Council (Geheimer Rat) was the inner circle of advisors to a ruler, representing the highest administrative authority.</note>, District Councilor, and Magistrate to His Reigning Serene Highness of Schleswig-Holstein-Gottorp;

->Of the<-

### High Cathedral Chapter of Lübeck, Canon <note>original: "Canonico." A canon was a high-ranking ecclesiastical official who helped oversee a cathedral.</note>, Hereditary Lord of Steinhorst, Tangstedt, Möislingen, Bramstedt, and Magnus-Word.

### To my highly favored
# Patron and great benefactor!

...extraordinary awakening of the Silesian Children's Prayer were certain precursors of the approaching <term>the great and terrible day of the LORD</term> <note>original Hebrew: "Yom YHWH ha-Gadol ve-ha-Nora"</note> and were so effective with GOD that they held Him back in the course of His great judgments, so that He might not come and strike the earth with a curse; indeed, that the very prayer of faithful and innocent children has the power to destroy the enemy and the vengeful. It is noteworthy that among other significant acts and intentions of the Silesian children, such tender children—once granted permission by the Breslau authorities <note>Breslau (modern-day Wrocław, Poland) was the center of this movement.</note> to pray in several churches alone—said of their own accord that they would continue to pray in the city and its churches until

until one should sing: **Christ is risen** <note>The traditional Easter hymn "Christ ist erstanden."</note> / which they also strictly observed. This they surely must have performed and represented in spirit as a prelude to the future great fulfillment—though the children themselves were unaware of it—showing how the prayer of the sighing and praying members of the church will then also cease when the time comes that the mystical Christ, made alive in the Spirit, goes forth and, in the sixth seal <note>A reference to the opening of the seals in the Book of Revelation.</note>, strips away the principalities and the powerful, who are still lords in the air, as well as the Antichrist, as the man of sin, and makes an end of him through the appearance of His coming. At this coming, He rises in the children of the first resurrection and hears their prayer; they, like the chosen of the LORD, have asked with that widow <note>A reference to the Parable of the Persistent Widow in Luke 18.</note> for **vindication** <note>original Greek: "ekdikēsin"</note>, and for deliverance from their enemies, as well as for an edification and expansion of His church as His household honor, and they will continue in prayer until they [reach] the mystical Easter Day of the New Testament.

<summary>Petersen interprets the Silesian children's prayer revival as a prophetic sign that delays God's judgment and foreshadows the "mystical Easter," where Christ triumphs over the Antichrist. He views the children's insistence on praying until the Easter hymn is sung as a symbolic act representing the future victory of the Church.</summary>

Now, although this treatise of mine, which deals with those Silesian children, has a lowly reputation among the world and worldly people, the matter is nonetheless so great in the eyes of GOD that He wished to announce through His prophets concerning those children at the end times, and thereby provide a sign <note>original: "Merckmahl"</note> from which the times of His second coming and His preceding judgments should be recognized. Since I have not learned of this children’s prayer movement through hearsay, nor solely from the writings of those who have written about it, but have heard it myself and seen it in many rural congregations, I have been sufficiently convinced that such a thing is not from men, nor from human arrangements, but from the Spirit

## Joh. Wilh. Petersen, D. <note>Johann Wilhelm Petersen (1649–1727) was a prominent theologian and a leader in the Pietist movement who was eventually removed from office for his millenarian views.</note>

## In the Name of JESUS! AMEN!

<margin>1707.</margin>
It would be highly irresponsible both toward the present and toward posterity / if we were to remain silent about the great and extraordinary event / that occurred with the children in Silesia / and did not wish to bring it to print. It is true that something of it has already been brought to light by others / but because so many have given a biased judgment / and / those who could have composed a better account / were led astray—partly out of the fear of men / and partly for other reasons—so that they have spoken far too little of it / while others on the contrary have wanted to attribute the work to <term>Enthusiasm</term> <note>In the 18th century, "Enthusiasm" (Enthusiasmus) was a derogatory term for religious fanaticism or the claim of receiving direct, private revelations from God.</note> / indeed even to the Devil / and his promptings; Thus I have resolved in the Name of GOD / following a previous close / and visible investigation / for which reason I among others traveled expressly to Silesia / to provide a true report of this. For although I was not present with all the congregations of the Silesian children /

<summary>Johann Wilhelm Petersen concludes his dedication and begins a formal defense of the 1707 Silesian children’s prayer movement, claiming his account is based on personal investigative travel to the region.</summary>
<keywords>Johann Wilhelm Petersen, Silesia, Children's Prayer Movement, Pietism, Enthusiasm, Magdeburg, 1707, religious controversy</keywords>

--- Page 13 ---
<meta>This page continues Petersen's account of the "Children’s Prayer" movement in Silesia. The catchword "thümern" on page 2 connects to "thümer" on page 3, completing the word "Fürstenthümer" (principalities).</meta>

-(0)-

...estates, of which there are a great number; yet from those I have seen and visited while traveling past, and having made diligent inquiry concerning the remaining children's congregations <note>German: *Kinder-Gemeinen*; groups of children gathering outdoors for prayer and song.</note> existing in one spirit, I have learned enough to know that the work did not begin without the special stirring of God the Holy Spirit, and was still being continued and carried forward when I traveled out again. Initially, the idea arose that these young people—prompted by the Convention <note>The Treaty of Altranstädt (1707) between Holy Roman Emperor Joseph I and King Charles XII of Sweden, which restored religious rights to Silesian Protestants.</note> between Their Imperial and Royal Majesties of Sweden, and the observation of the prayers which the Swedish soldiers performed in the middle of the field—wished to follow such a laudable work in their own prayers. We do not entirely wish to deny this; but who would doubt (as one has reasoned quite well) that since everything among the children is maintained in such beautiful order, with fervent zeal, and despite all obstacles, it is not only constantly sustained but also spreads further and further? Who would not confess that it must have its first origin in a higher impulse than merely a habit learned from the Swedes? Indeed, one has so much credible testimony from so many sensible people who attended this established children's devotion, that it can hardly be viewed without a special stirring of the soul. In particular, it is very well worth observing, and should be taken deeply to heart, that within about five days, in five principalities <note>Silesia was divided into various duchies and principalities.</note>

<column-break/>

-(0)-

of the land of Silesia, the prayer of the children started up simultaneously and so swiftly, as if a sudden storm-wind and hurricane <note>The author uses "Orcan" (hurricane) to describe the sudden, overwhelming nature of the spiritual revival.</note> had arisen, and they were moved to it with a collective hand. We cannot conceive of this without a hidden Divinity providing the impulse. I must, however, marvel that while the modern world and worldly-minded priests otherwise think nothing at all of miracles—and are ready to say of every special and extraordinary work of God in the revelation of His wonders through His servants and handmaids that "miracles have now ceased"—they now insist on miracles regarding the children's prayer. They object, asking what special thing one actually sees in the children, for they have not yet performed any miracles. As if it were not miracle enough that this age group, otherwise flighty and scattered in its thoughts, has maintained its prayer-practice so constantly, diligently, and orderly, and so precisely, in winter and summer, in good and bad weather! They have held to it with great devotion and have sought, and still seek, the face of God against all the threats of parents and tutors <note>Original: *Præceptoren*.</note>, of priests, of bailiffs, and of the authorities, even when threatened with being thrown into the dungeon, being whipped, or being made into soldiers as they grew older. And although they do not yet pray from their hearts or perform such deeds as God is accustomed to perform through youths and men in Christ, yet it is

<summary>Petersen argues that the sudden, widespread, and disciplined prayer movement among Silesian children is a divine miracle, despite skeptics who demand supernatural signs. He highlights the children's resilience against severe threats of punishment from parents and authorities.</summary>

<keywords>Silesia, children's prayer movement, Altranstädt Convention, Swedish soldiers, Pietism, religious persecution, miracles, spiritual awakening</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the theological defense of the "Children’s Prayer" movement. The previous page ended by noting that the movement's rapid spread across five principalities suggests a divine origin rather than mere imitation of Swedish soldiers.</meta>

<page-num>4</page-num>
<header>*(0)*</header>

it is yet known that God condescends <note>German: *condescendire*; a theological term meaning God stoops down to reach human understanding.</note> according to the measure and portion of each person; he becomes, as it were, a youth among youths, and deals with and works through children in a childlike manner.

§. 2. How can the great God ever satisfy the world’s desires so that they do not always find something to criticize in His works? But He does not let such things lead Him astray; rather, He continues in His deeds and purposes, and will surely justify Himself. He shall be justified by His children of wisdom against the children of the world when posterity learns from the judgment why God stirred these children in such a way, and made this otherwise immature <note>German: *unmündige*; literally "under-aged" or "without a voice," referring here to those without legal or social standing.</note> people mature. It has often pleased this holy and wonderful God to do great things and put the clever of the world to shame, not only through the "mystical" little ones—those who, by renouncing all their own high worldly wisdom, have recognized the hidden wisdom through the anointing of the Spirit in sincerity <note>Original Greek: *εἰλικρίνεια* (eilikrineia); meaning moral purity or sincerity.</note>, simplicity, and purity of heart—but it has also been His good pleasure to appear wonderfully and work through those who are immature in years, and thus truly in small children. Through the grace of God, they are just as capable of this anointing in such years as the adults whom God deems worthy of it; indeed, they are often much more capable than others, because adults often resist through their natural reasoning and through

<meta>catchword: through</meta>

<column-break/>

<page-num>5</page-num>
<header>*(0)*</header>

through their human reason. In contrast, little children, who do not resist, accept what God works in the simplicity of their hearts in a childlike way and without over-subtle brooding. Who would dispute the radiance of these children, whose angels always behold the face of their Heavenly Father? If they have faith, why should they not please God through faith, and through faith do such things that are divine and pleasing to God? Even John [the Baptist], while still in his mother’s womb, lived in faith through the Spirit moving within him, and showed honor to Christ his Savior with his leaping and rejoicing when the faithful Elizabeth heard Mary's greeting. Why then should the Spirit of the Lord not be able to work in a child who is already out of the mother's womb? 

"Generally," writes a profound mystical teacher, "children are not hindered, nor distracted by any evil enticement; they strive and labor to return to that place from whence they came. Hence, in my opinion, the Greatest Doctor said: unless you become as little children, you shall not enter into the Kingdom of Heaven; for namely, the souls of children contend to return there before they are infected [by sin]." <note>Original Latin: *Communiter Pueri non sunt impediti, nec aliquo malo à lenocinio distracti, affectant & satagunt, redire illuc, unde venerunt. Hinc, ut opinor, summus Doctor dixit, nisi efficiamini, sicut parvuli, non intrabitis in Regnum cœlorum, quia videlicet illuc redire contendunt puerorum animæ, antequam inficiantur.*</note> <term>Francesco Giorgio, *Sacred Problems*, Volume 6, Section 3, Problem 101, Paris Edition, folio 393.</term> Bernard of Clairvaux, in his book *On the Order of Life*, derives the word for child <note>Latin: *Puer*.</note> from "purity" <note>Latin: *puritas*.</note> and says that simplicity, innocence, and purity reign in a child. "Children are named for their purity, and it is fitting that simplicity, innocence, and puri-" <note>Original Latin: *Pueri à puritate dicti sunt, & decet, ut regnent in eis simplicitas, & innocentia, & puri-*</note>

<meta>catchword: puri-</meta>

He supports this using biblical examples like John the Baptist and citations from mystical scholars like Francesco Giorgio and St. Bernard.</summary>

purity, that is, virginity <note>original: "puritas, hoc est, virginitas"</note>. According to the histories of the Church, one must marvel at how even tender children went with joy to their martyr’s crowns like lambs and little sheep for the slaughter. And how Origen <note>An early Christian theologian (c. 184–253 AD) known for his extreme devotion; as a youth, he reportedly wanted to join his father in martyrdom.</note> in his most tender youth possessed such love and fervor for suffering that they could hardly restrain him from it? Who worked such things in them? Is it not the very same God? To Him, it is the same whether He works His deeds and reveals His glory in adults or in small children. Indeed, His glory becomes more visible in children than in adults, of whom one might think (as some indeed have done) that they endured such martyrdom out of a firm resolve or a whim <note>German: *caprise*; an old spelling of caprice.</note>, seeking some personal glory through their own strength. Such objections—which the General of Martyrs <note>A poetic reference to Christ as the leader of the faithful.</note> oversees—cease when it comes to tender children.

§ 3. Now that God has made a beginning of this work through the Silesian children <note>Referring to the "Children’s Prayer" (*Kinderbeten*) movement in Silesia (1707–1708), where thousands of children gathered in fields to pray for peace.</note> in the Silesian mountains—and indeed, as reported from Great Glogau <note>Modern Głogów, Poland.</note> on January 2nd, 1708, this occurred afterward in Sprottau <note>Modern Szprotawa, Poland.</note> on the Day of the Holy Innocents <note>December 28th; a feast day commemorating the children killed by Herod, here used to link the movement to biblical themes of childhood innocence.</note> of the previous year—it has spread from one place to another in such a visible manner, working such unusual zeal for prayer in them. For we believe, even according to common theological propositions <note>German: *Thesibus*.</note>, that since they were born of Christian parents and were received and accepted into God’s covenant through Holy Baptism,

<meta>catchword: merged /</meta>

Miracles have otherwise happened here and there in many past years, both in the heavens, on the earth, and in the sea. A few years ago, we also had many earthquakes and gazed with astonishment at the very great Comet of the year 1680 <note>The Great Comet of 1680 was one of the brightest of the 17th century, often interpreted as a providential sign or warning.</note>. But how has the world come to God through these things? Have people not remained as hardened as before? But now that God takes the children, as the parents' own flesh and blood, and moves them so powerfully to prayer, He has prepared an open door for Himself to enter the parents' hearts, moving them more conveniently and powerfully. Our God has succeeded in this, having brought many hundreds of parents to different thoughts and drawn them to Himself through these means. From such a great divine effect, we can surely conclude that the cause must also be divine. As I have seen with my own eyes—what movements occurred in the hearts of the elders during the prayer and after the prayer was finished, both under the open sky and at home—and whoever saw how their eyes were full of tears, he will [dismiss] all

...one can easily refute the objections made by adversaries. All these things are confirmed not only from Great Glogau <note>Modern Głogów, Poland.</note> from January 2nd, 1708, but also from Liegnitz <note>Modern Legnica, Poland.</note>, from Breslau <note>Modern Wrocław, Poland.</note>, and Lemberg <note>Modern Lviv, Ukraine.</note>, as can be read at length in the tract, which is printed in folio and is approximately four sheets large. But particularly noteworthy is the circumstance—noted even by an enemy of the praying children—that the largest crowd of the praying children are mostly simple and of those years in which they cannot yet have left the German primary schools <note>German: *Teutschen Lehr-Schulen*; these provided basic education in the vernacular, indicating the children were very young, likely under 12 or 13.</note>. One sometimes watched them, not without a stirring of the soul, pairing up quietly and walking quite orderly out of the city. There, at a place chosen by them, they would form a circle by modestly joining hands with one another. Then they would sing a morning or evening hymn appropriate to the time, and further, one of them—who acted as a priest and was generally the oldest, and perhaps the most sensible—would read a Psalm, and they would pray several prayers known to them and in themselves quite irreproachable. After completing the singing of the hymns, they would part from one another modestly and quietly to go home. They, the parents, and other elders themselves confess that in the previous years of their lives, neither through sermons nor otherwise, have they had such fervor and stirring of their hearts as they have received through the praying children; whose power—which the Spirit of God has shown in them—they have not been able to re-

<meta>catchword: re-</meta>

-sist, even though they resisted with all their might, partly of their own accord and partly incited by others who held positions of authority. Truly, such an example of persistent prayer by children (an act in which even the Saints often become sluggish) has never been heard of or read about in any province of the world. Should one regard this as a small thing? Or, as some priests have done, reject it entirely and attribute it to the Devil? I would not take all the world’s goods and hold such thoughts, much less pass such a vile judgment as those insolent spirits have passed.

§ 4. Even if nothing were promised in Holy Scripture regarding this grace of prayer foretold for the end times for both old and young, it would still be highly necessary at all times to practice prayer by virtue of the general command to seek the face of God in prayer. But if a special promise is now present through the revelation of God in His words, and such a beginning and dawning is actually taking place, who then is so bold and reckless as to blaspheme against it? For one should take the argument from exactly such stirrings of the Holy Spirit that the signs of the end times are already at the door, and that we therefore have the highest cause to worthily prepare ourselves for the approaching King of Glory and to go out to meet Him as well-adorned brides <note>A biblical reference to the Parable of the Ten Virgins (Matthew 25), symbolizing the Church's readiness for the return of Christ.</note>. § 8. The text in the prophet is quite significant, which reads: *Behold! I will send you the prophet Elijah before the great and terrible day of the LORD comes; he shall turn the heart of the fathers to the children, and the heart of the children to their fathers, so that I do not come and strike the earth with a curse.* <note>Malachi 4:5–6.</note> Here, above all things, one must investigate what time **Malachi** is speaking of. This can be recognized from the words included in the text: that the speech is not actually about the time of the beginning of the New Testament, when **Christ** came in the flesh at His first arrival, but rather about the time when He will come at His second arrival for judgment, to judge the living and the dead. For when **Christ** came in the flesh, He did not come to judge the world, but that the world through Him might be saved (John 3:17). Then He was sacrificed <term>once</term> <note>original Greek: *ἅπαξ* (hapax)</note> for all time to take away the sins of many; but when He comes <term>for the second time</term> <note>original Greek: *ἐκ δευτέρου* (ek deuterou)</note> at His—

You shall tread down the godless, for they shall be as ashes under your feet on the day that I shall make," says the Lord of Hosts. not a small amount would be lost; indeed, that their authority would be considerably diminished if the listeners noticed that they were so greatly moved in the presence of the children and preferred toward God—something they had not perceived in so many sermons preached in the churches, year in and year out. Maidens who are taught by **GOD** shall come, and they shall receive from the **LORD** the wisdom of the Fisherman of the New Testament <note>A reference to the "apostolic simplicity" of Peter and the other apostles, who were common laborers rather than trained scholars</note>—which is promised to the innocent little children in the spirit for the end times. They shall cast into a heap the self-chosen forms of worship <note>original: *selbst-erwählte Gottesdienste*; a critique of religious rituals created by human tradition rather than divine command</note> together with the falsely-famed arts <note>original: *falsche berühmte Kunst*; referring to the complex scholastic theology taught in universities</note>, so that **GOD** and His Word may arise and remain for eternity.

And such shall happen according to the prophecy of the eighth Psalm, which—according to the second chapter of the Letter to the Hebrews—pertains not only to Christ, but also to the end times, wherein the victory of young children and infants against the enemy and the vengeful is witnessed. That this Psalm, however, pertains primarily to the end times is immediately clear and bright from the first verse, where it says: **LORD** our Ruler, how glorious is Your name in all lands! This glorious name of **GOD** was neither glorious in all lands in David’s time, nor is it in our time, in which the world is mostly full of wickedness and injustice, and the prince of this world <note>A biblical title for Satan</note>—who does his work in the children of unbelief—rages and storms. 

But when the earth shall be full of the knowledge of the **LORD**, as the sea is covered with water <note>original: *See-Wasser*; a reference to Isaiah 11:9</note>, and the **LORD** shall be one, and His name only one, then the kingdoms of our **GOD** shall belong to His Christ, and the **LORD** shall be King over all lands. **Zechariah 14:9; Revelation 11:15–17; Isaiah 11:9.** Which King David saw beforehand in the spirit, and in the spirit spoke out—

<meta>catchword: out—</meta>

<page-num>39</page-num>
<header>*(0)*</header>

—spoken and at the same time prophesied how he would see the "heavens, the work of Your fingers, the moon and the stars, which **GOD** has prepared." He indeed saw the moon and the stars in the sky that we also see; however, because he speaks here of something in the future, it refers to the new heaven and the new earth in which righteousness dwells, for which Peter also waited according to the promise of **GOD**, and for which we with him still wait. Over this, David rejoices in the spirit: that he shall get to see them and be glad. 

When he beheld in the spirit of faith the deep humiliation <note>original: *Erniedrigung*; a theological term for Christ's suffering and earthly life as a servant</note> of the dear Savior, he saw no glory in such a work of redemption and in His great sufferings, at the time when He was forsaken by **GOD** for a little while. But when he looked through the sufferings toward the glories that would follow, it did not hinder him from seeing the kingdom of Jesus Christ, the holy Messiah; rather, he immediately added: "You will crown him with honor and adornment; You will make him Lord over the work of Your hands—sheep and oxen altogether, the birds under the heaven, and the fish in the sea, and whatever passes through the sea." 

While this has indeed already happened in a certain sense through the glorious Ascension, when He seated Himself at the right hand of His Father and fulfilled all things, it is nevertheless still very much unknown to most people and creatures. His glory is also not yet seen in this way, which Paul himself confesses in the Letter to the Hebrews, Chapter 2, verse 8, when he says:

strength: *Out of the mouth of young children and infants.* <note>original Hebrew: מפי עוללים וינקים; original Greek: ἐκ στόματος νηπίων καὶ θηλαζόντων. These are children who are small in years, yet enlightened by **GOD** to be used for His work. Telesphorus <note>Telesphorus of Cosenza was a 14th-century hermit and prophetic writer whose works were often cited during the Reformation to support claims of church renewal.</note> understood this, and in his commentary on the first chapter of Jeremiah, he wrote as follows: *Just as God once chose the older Fathers, and secondly the younger Apostles: so now He will also choose children in the literal sense, specifically for the sake of those to whom the old things have become worthless, to preach the Gospel of the Kingdom, and to rescue Lot in those same faithful children through Abraham the High Priest.* <note>original Latin: "Sicut olim elegit Deus Patres seniores, secundo Apostolos juniores: ita & nunc eliget PUEROS AD LITERAM, propter eos, quibus videlicet vetera viluerunt, ad prædicandum REGNI EVANGELIUM, & excutiendum Loth in ipsis pueris fidelibus per Abraham summum Pontificem."</note> That is to say: Just as **God** in ancient times chose the Patriarchs, but after them the Apostles, who were younger; so now He will choose children, understood according to the literal sense and thus according to their age. For just as the children of Israel who were born in the desert were still saved and brought by **God** into the Promised Land, while all their fathers—except for Joshua and Caleb, in whom there was a different spirit—were killed and struck down; so **God** will also yet save these awakened, faithful children along with Lot. Rather, as it is otherwise said: **Prayers are the weapons of the Church** <note>original Latin: "Preces sunt arma Ecclesiæ"</note>, so they arm themselves with prayer and pray for the Emperor and for freedom of conscience, so that the Word of God may run among them and come into full swing. It is also well known in Church history what occurred at the time of Athanasius <note>Athanasius the Great (c. 296–373 AD) was a prominent bishop and theologian in Alexandria.</note> regarding children. For when Bishop Alexander of Alexandria celebrated the feast day of the martyr Peter, and after performing his duties was waiting at a place near the sea for the clergy whom he had invited to eat with him, he saw from a distance on the seashore a game played by boys. They were acting as bishops usually do, performing the things that usually happen in the church. As he watched the boys for a long time, he saw that they were even imitating certain spiritual actions. He was troubled by this and hurriedly summoned the clergy to him, commanding them to look and quickly bring all the boys to him. When they arrived, he asked them what they had been doing. At first, they were frightened, but afterward they recounted everything in order and confessed that they had baptized several Christian students, called <term>catechumens</term> <note>individuals receiving instruction in the basics of the faith before baptism</note>, and that this was done through Athanasius, who had been made bishop in this church-play—

When the Catholic boy subsequently left the Lutheran, the completed church remained with the Lutheran boy. And because the former had been older, the latter had to accommodate his wishes regarding the altars; thus, the interior is built in the Catholic fashion with a high altar and various small altars. One must marvel not a little that they observed the most accurate rules of architecture so well, and also forgot nothing in the interior that is found in a large church—indeed, even the lamp was hung before the high altar. On the outside, it is built with bricks according to the old <term>Gothic architecture</term>. He made the bells out of glass.

When I and another companion, who took me into Silesia in his carriage, had the boy who built the church come to us and asked him to ring the bells, he rang them with such dexterity and skill that it was a wonder to see, and he continues this ringing every morning and evening when they ring in the Catholic churches. It is also remarkable that while the children in Sagan performed their usual prayer to God in their closed circle, in the midst of such prayer, one bird—and at another time two birds—flew around, like large pigeons with small heads and black beaks, upon whose breasts a shining ray shimmered, presenting a golden-yellow spot. The first bird came from the North <note>Original: "Norden".</note> and always flew around the children at the hour they prayed, almost over their heads; the others also came from the North <note>Original: "Mitternacht", literally "midnight," an archaic term for North.</note> and likewise flew around the children, and did not show themselves further, but departed toward the South <note>Original: "Mittag", literally "midday," an archaic term for South.</note>.

We have heard all these circumstances not merely from one or another person, but from many who saw these dove-like birds with their own eyes, in the same manner and with all the same details. This is recounted with even more detail in the reports attached to the impartial opinion of Inspector Neumann in folio: Adam Rauben, a carter from Sagan in Silesia—an honest man—says that when the children in Sagan (one and a half miles from Sorau) held their **prayer hours** <note>These "Bet-Stunden" were spontaneous, open-air prayer meetings led by children, which became a widespread phenomenon in Silesia during this period.</note> in the open fields next to the city this past Tuesday, February 14, 1708—as is also being done by children in other Silesian cities and villages—a white bird with a black beak and red feet, with a breast that shone like a small ground-bird and was somewhat larger than a dove, flew right over the children and onto the Jesuit College, where it disappeared. Afterward, on Thursday the 16th of this month, when both old and young attended the prayer hours with great devotion, they had barely sung the first lines: **"Oh God, look down from heaven and have mercy!"** <note>original: "Ach GOtt von Himmel sieh darein / und laß dich doch erbarmen!" The same thing happened again yesterday, Friday evening, the 17th of the same month, with such a bird, which several hundred people watched with great wonder and tears. It is remarkable—and a constant truth against Master Scharff in the futile appendix to his writing—that a boy who has a poor father (and whose father had wanted to keep him from the prayers) found a <term>ducat</term> <note>A gold coin used as a standard currency throughout Europe at the time.</note> at the place where he knelt with the others. He showed it to his father, saying that he could now well buy what was necessary. This ducat, which God bestowed upon the boy and which the local preacher (whom I know well) redeemed, still lies well-preserved with the aforementioned priest, who told me this himself. In the meantime, we went to the Jesuit College in Sagan and asked the Jesuits what they thought of the prayers of such children; they revealed enough that they had to confess it was something **extraordinary** <note>original: "extraordinaires"</note>, yet they did not want to attribute it to the devil at all, as it has been so shamefully judged by some Lutheran priests.

<meta>This page continues the eyewitness account of the Pietist children's prayer movement in the early 18th century. The previous page detailed supernatural sightings of white birds in Sagan, which were interpreted as divine signs supporting the children’s spontaneous outdoor prayer meetings.</meta>

Attempts were indeed made through the Council to prevent the children's prayer there; however, the boy who had first begun the prayer told the **administrator** <note>original: "Verweser" - a local official or deputy governor</note>—who had summoned him before the office—that prayer was certainly not evil. He said it would be against his conscience if he were to cease; they had previously been raised **like cattle**, so why should they not now pray for the churches and schools?

§. 18. When we arrived in **Sorau** <note>Now Żary, Poland; at the time, it was a major city in Lower Lusatia, bordering Silesia.</note>, I was told how the children there, already some weeks prior, had prayed quietly in their houses and had built an **altar**. Upon this altar, they placed six thought-provoking painted images, which they had painted themselves as best they could. I saw these with my own eyes and wondered greatly what concepts they must have had in mind when they produced such images and placed them upon the altar.

The first image was a large church. The second, a **dreadful owl** <note>In the symbolism of the time, the owl often represented spiritual darkness or those who "shun the light" of the Gospel.</note>. The third, the **Saxon coat of arms** and a flag in which two stars could be seen. The fourth, a very bloody man who had a saber at his side and a crown upon his head. The fifth, a crowned man with two eagle’s wings upon his back. The sixth, a sun with nine rays of various colors. 

The children and their parents were brought before the **Consistory** <term>Consistory: A church administrative body or court that handled matters of discipline and doctrine.</term> and were threatened that if they did not cease such prayer meetings, both parties would be harshly punished; however, the children

<page-num>61</page-num>
<header>*(0)*</header>
<meta>catchword: to</meta>

are said to have defended themselves well. It was alleged that the children had administered the **Lord’s Supper** <note>The Sacrament of Communion; for children to perform this without a priest would have been considered a grave theological and legal offense.</note> to one another, but this was found to be false. 

The priests there are said to argue that the children's praying in **Silesia** was of an entirely different nature than the children's praying in Sorau. They argued that because the people in Silesia had no churches <note>Following the Counter-Reformation, many Protestant churches in Silesia had been confiscated by the Catholic authorities, forcing Protestants to worship in fields or "Peace Churches."</note>, one could not prevent them from praying or interpret it poorly. But since no churches were under such pressure in their own location, this praying could not be from God and His Spirit—as if the **Preaching Office** <note>The formal, ordained ministry.</note> were insulted by the Holy Spirit if He moved children to prayer outside of such external churches. 

In particular, one preacher—who had harbored great enmity toward the late **Mr. M. Böse** <note>Refers to Magister Böse, a Pietist clergyman known for his controversial spiritual fervor.</note>—behaved offensively and sent word to the **Co-Rector** <note>An assistant headmaster or school official.</note> to punish the children for it. As long as the prayer movement was occurring only in Silesia, people could tolerate it and say that God's hand and finger were at work; but as it drew closer and broke through even in places where there were no persecuted congregations, it quickly became "an evil thing." They claimed it could not be from the Holy Spirit and that the **holy ministry** <note>original: "heilige Ministerium" - the collective body of ordained clergy.</note> was being insulted and despised by it. 

When I arrived in **Malmitz**, which belongs to the **Count von Reder**, I encountered the children under the open sky; subsequently, I also found such praying congregations of children in **Dittersbach**, which belongs to **Count Reuss, Henry XXIV**.

<summary>The author describes the spread of the Children’s Prayer movement to Sorau and the authorities' hostile reaction, including a description of symbolic folk art created by the children and the theological arguments used by the clergy to suppress the movement.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the eyewitness account of the Pietist children's prayer movement in Silesia. The narrator describes his direct observations of children in various villages gathering to pray and sing, moving adults to tears with their unexpected piety.</meta>

<page-num>62</page-num>
<header>-(0)-</header>

witnessed with amazement. I cannot sufficiently describe the devotion which not only the small children, but also the standing elders—who were awakened by the children—testified toward almighty God. With such order, zeal, sighing, and longing—the likes of which have never before been seen among the youth in the villages—did the children pray. This drew tears from my own eyes, as well as those of the strangers present; one never left such a scene without great edification and a sense of offering oneself up to God. 

They mostly sing songs of repentance <note>original: "Buß-Lieder" — hymns focused on penitence and the acknowledgement of sin</note>, and such songs as are fitting for children. Upon this, they fall down upon their knees and repeat the words: **Lord, have mercy on us! Christ Jesus, have mercy on us! Lord God Holy Spirit, have mercy on us!** <note>These phrases are the "Kyrie eleison," "Christe eleison," and "Kyrie eleison," a standard liturgical prayer for mercy.</note> These words, as one could hear and feel, came from the innermost depths of their hearts and were pronounced with an emphasis almost beyond the natural manner of children. They also have certain fine prayers which they pray on their knees with fixed, steady eyes, then they sing another song and conclude their devotion with a silent Lord's Prayer. Then, without shouting, playing, or joking—as if they were elderly men—they go home. The boys form a half-circle and the girls form the other; the elderly women stood together in a group, while several steps away stood the men. In the village of

<column-break/>

<page-num>63</page-num>
<header>-(0)-</header>

**Malmitz** <note>Now Małomice, Poland.</note> they began their hour early at 10:00 AM, and at midday at 4:00 PM when they came out of school. The boy who reads aloud is exceptional in his way, as is the boy in **Dittersbach** <note>Likely Dzietrzychowice, a village near Sorau.</note>, as they read the prayers slowly and distinctly and begin the melodies. They do the same in other places, though with the difference that they change the hour as it suits them; in some places they gather for prayer three times, in some twice, and in some only once. 

Not far from **Sorau** <note>Now Żary, Poland.</note>, in the prayer hours, they have read the History of Bel in Babylon <note>original: "Historiam von dem Bel zu Babel" — referring to the story of "Bel and the Dragon," a deuterocanonical addition to the Book of Daniel in which the prophet Daniel exposes the fraud of a pagan idol.</note>, and in other places, other thought-provoking texts. From this, one can well see what a love they have for God’s Word and how they choose that which they deem useful to read. I have seen very many of these congregations, but in the entire **Duchy of Silesia**, there are said to be very many who will not allow themselves to be disturbed in any way—even though in **Breslau** <note>Now Wrocław, Poland.</note>, where it started last, they first tried to prevent it with force. Nevertheless, one church still remains where they come together and perform their prayers. In **Sprottau** <note>Now Szprotawa, Poland.</note>, the prayer is also said to be hindered, and in other places the carnal priests <note>original: "fleischlichen Priester" — unspiritual or worldly clergy who opposed the emotional, lay-led Pietist movement.</note> are very intent on abolishing it. They present the children with captious <note>intended to entrap or confuse</note> questions and examine them on which part of their **Catechism** <term>Catechism: A summary of religious doctrine used for instruction, often in a question-and-answer format.</term> this or that law in their song and prayer belonged to, and other such things. But it goes here as the poet says: <note>original: "Sed..." — Latin for "But..."</note>

<summary>An eyewitness account of the Silesian children's prayer movement, describing their orderly and emotional worship services in villages like Malmitz and Dittersbach, as well as the growing opposition from worldly church authorities.</summary>

<page-num>64</page-num>
<header>(0)</header>
<meta>catchword: che (completing the word "solche" or "such")</meta>

->*But their fathers forbade it, what they could not forbid.*<- <note>original Latin: "Sed vetuere Patres, quod non potuere vetare." A quote from Ovid's *Metamorphoses* regarding the lovers Pyramus and Thisbe, used here to suggest that the children's inner drive for prayer is stronger than any parental or official prohibition.</note>

For they do not allow themselves to be deterred from their fiery prayer; which they have, without doubt, received according to their part and measure from the holy fire of the golden altar. Neither wetness nor heat, neither summer nor winter nor snow keeps them from it; instead, they tie a little straw together and take it with them onto the wet field or onto the snow, and then kneel upon it and pray. If an Emperor, King, or Prince, or some other authority were to issue a command that people should come together two or three times throughout the summer and winter and pray as these children pray, one would see how sluggish and how difficult it would be to achieve. But here, where no such command exists and the children have become a law unto themselves—to which they hold so firmly and diligently—one sees that it comes from the free spirit of the Gospel, which drives them forth on the wings of the wind of God, so that they fly up in their prayers like eagles and do not grow weary.

From **Great Glogau** <note>Now Głogów, Poland</note> it is written on January 2nd, 1708, that the magistrate drove the children away three times from the place where they held their prayer hours. However, they moved to the so-called **Fiscal-Berg** <note>Tax Hill; an area of land under imperial or treasury jurisdiction rather than municipal control</note>, which is a "freedom" <term>Freyheit: a place with special legal status where the city council has no authority</term> where the magistrate has no power to command. The place is cordoned off with poles so that they are not crowded by the mass of people coming there daily, as more than 3,000 to 4,000 people have been counted coming there to witness such

<column-break/>

<page-num>65</page-num>
<header>(0)</header>
<sig>E</sig>
<meta>catchword: sich</meta>

extraordinary devotion from tender children. The children kneel on the earth for the entire duration of the prayer hours; they have chosen a reader from among themselves; they sing, and pray, and read a chapter from the Bible. Finally, the children all at once lift their folded hands high and sing: *O thou great GOD, hear us!* and conclude with the hymn: *Now God be praised, it is finished!* It is unbelievable what extraordinary devotion these boys display, and how everything proceeds so orderly, as they take each other by the hand in pairs when leaving. The people standing around cannot watch without tears and sing along with them, which creates such a sound that it can be heard almost a quarter-mile away on the **Oder** <note>The major river flowing through Silesia</note>. 

This is also written from **Liegnitz** <note>Now Legnica, Poland</note> on February 5th. From **Breslau** <note>Now Wrocław, Poland</note>, someone reports that children from age 4 to 12, and finally up to 14, from many towns and villages form a circle under the open sky, kneel down and pray, and sing songs of repentance <note>original: "Buß-Lieder"</note>. No one knows how the children—even without the parents' prior knowledge or will—fell into such an impulse. From the same city of **Breslau**, on February 22nd, it is reported that an indescribable multitude of people attended the children's prayer before the **Nicholas Gate**; the **Baron von Strahlenheim**, the Royal Swedish Commissioner, was also there. They come together three times a day and pray heartily, which one of them in the middle...

<page-num>66</page-num>

<meta>catchword: da</meta>

a boy present there, whom they have chosen for this purpose, reads aloud. Both Lutheran and Catholic children join together, and they know nothing of the mutual condemnation in which one party is generally accustomed to damn the other. It also happened that a Jewish child approached the praying children; when one of the Christian children said that the child did not belong with them, the Jewish child answered: **Christ** died for them as well. Upon hearing this, one of the Christian children replied very thoughtfully: "Thus we see that God has also opened a door to the Jews and the heathens!" When the children were summoned before the magistrate, they answered the questions put to them so sensibly and wisely that everyone was filled with wonder. The children depart from one another very quietly and in good order—as is reported from Lemberg <note>Likely Löwenberg in Silesia, now Lwówek Śląski, Poland</note>—just as other children are accustomed to do when they leave their lessons. Even when the weather was poor, they did not allow their devotion to be hindered; instead, they held their hats over their books so they would not get wet and even brought a little straw along, upon which they knelt during wet weather. This, then, is a true account of the order and conduct of the praying children.

§. 19. Now we wish to point out what stirrings and movements are occurring among children in other kingdoms, from which one may recognize that this is a general, holy uprising and awakening which God the Lord, through His Spirit, has awakened here and there.

I include here an account taken from a small treatise arriving from England, which will shed considerable light on these matters. The French book is titled: **Sacred Theater of the Cevennes, printed in London 1707.** <note>Original title: *Theatre Sacre de Cevennes*. This book documented the prophetic and ecstatic experiences of the Camisards, French Protestants who rebelled against Louis XIV.</note> For this work, the widely famous and learned Mr. Misson <note>François-Maximilien Misson, a French Huguenot writer who lived in exile in London.</note> wrote an extensive preface in which he strongly defends these people. He praises them as honest and upright folk and expresses great compassion for them, noting that in London they must live as sheep among wolves. The booklet itself consists entirely of testimonies regarding the extraordinary events that occurred with the Camisards <note>A group of Huguenot insurgents in the Cevennes mountains of France.</note> in the Cevennes. Through these accounts, the aforementioned Misson wishes to give credit to those currently in England, showing that they did not manufacture or feign their <term>ecstatic movements<note>Original Latin: *motus ecstaticos*; referring to the physical trances, trembling, or seizures experienced during spiritual inspiration.</note></term>, since such things had previously been so common in both the Dauphiné and the Cevennes. These testimonies are of three kinds: the first are drawn from printed writings, such as the *History of the Edict of Nantes*, printed in Delft in 1695, and the Papist Beneys' *History of the Fanaticism of Our Time*, and others. The second are from the private letters of trustworthy people; and the third are testimonies given in London by twenty-six <term>eyewitnesses<note>Original Latin: *Oculatis testibus*.</note></term> and finally confirmed. From all of these, it can be seen that these <term>inspirations<note>Original Latin: *Inspirationes*.</note></term> were first noticed in the year 1688 in the Dauphiné among the newly converted, and in a short time increased to such an extent that,

<meta>Continuing from the description of the praying children in Silesia, the author now shifts focus to a similar religious movement occurring in the Cevennes region of France, where children are reportedly exhibiting prophetic abilities under intense persecution.</meta>

<page-num>68</page-num>
<header> (0) </header>
<meta>catchword: spoken-</meta>

As Monsieur Beneys <note>likely referring to Elie Benoist, a historian who documented the Huguenots</note>, a great enemy of these people, says: "No fire, when driven by the wind, runs so quickly from one house to another as this madness flew from one parish to the next." He notes that an unspeakable multitude of "little prophets" or prophesying children arose, and such wondrous things happened among them that no person could believe it if it had not been seen in the sight of all France. These events were confirmed by so many parliamentary decrees, <term>intendants</term> <note>high-ranking administrative officials in France</note>, commanders, judicial acts, and other justifications and reports that no reasonable person could doubt them. 

However, even though this fire was somewhat dampened by the enemies' gruesome persecution, it is said to have broken out again in the Cevennes <note>a mountainous region in southern France</note> with even greater intensity. The beginning occurred among the children, among whom an extraordinary awakening and urge to pray is said to have arisen. During prayer, they supposedly fall into ecstasies <note>original: "Ecstases"</note>, and in these trances, they preach repentance and exhort their parents to turn their hearts to **GOD** and no longer defile themselves with <term>Papist</term> <note>a derogatory term used at the time for Roman Catholics</note> abominations. It is said that even children of only 14 months have done this; to the astonishment of all present, while sitting on the lap or at the breast of their mother, they gave powerful exhortations to repentance (following the fulfillment of the eighth Psalm and the prophet Malachi in the 6th chapter <note>Original text cites Malachi VI, though the book usually has only four chapters; this likely refers to chapter 4</note>) and 

<column-break/>

<page-num>69</page-num>
<header> (0) </header>
<sig>E 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: are</meta>

spoke in distinct speech, even though outside of the ecstasy <note>original: "Entzückung"</note> they could not speak well. Because of this, they were treated gruesomely by the Papists and miserably beaten with rods, burned under the soles of their feet, and otherwise tormented <note>original: "tormentiret"</note> so that they might say they had been instructed by someone. The physicians <note>original: "Medici"</note> also had to examine them. Regardless, such movements <note>original: "Motus"</note> increased from day to day and caused a general stirring even among the adults, who likewise received such inspirations <note>original: "Inspirationes"</note>. 

With this, the persecution also increased, and all the prisons were filled with these people, until finally orders were given by the Court to no longer arrest such people but to massacre them immediately wherever they might be found. They then took up arms upon a presumed command from God, spoken through the mouth of a pious peasant, from which the well-known internal troubles <note>referring to the Camisard Rebellion</note> subsequently arose. However, they continued their meetings most zealously in the wildernesses and forests, for which the location was commonly named by the Spirit. Often others, who did not know the location or could not find it, were led there by a light which appeared as a star in the air. In these meetings, once again, some—both great and small, of both sexes—were seized. In a paroxysm <note>original: "Paroxysmo," meaning a sudden fit or seizure</note> often lasting two whole hours, they spoke in the most beautiful connection and with such convincing power that even the most daring people were moved by it and turned to **GOD**.

It details how these supernatural events led to the Camisard uprising and clandestine religious gatherings in the wilderness.</summary>

were converted / many of whom later received the inspirations themselves / as happened, for example, to the Cavalier / a father of the leader of the Camisards <note>The Camisards were Huguenot (Protestant) insurgents in the Cevennes mountains who rose up against the persecutions of King Louis XIV</note> / who, as an insolent youth, came to a meeting merely out of curiosity <note>original: "par curiosité"</note> / but there, through two boys / who spoke to him while in ecstasy / and knew and rebuked all the thoughts arising in his mind / was so humbled and made small / that he recognized his miserable state of sin / and felt an uncommon sadness and anxiety over it / until **GOD** comforted him again / and graced him with the gift of inspiration <note>original: "dono Inspirationis"</note>. All the plots of their enemies were revealed to them through the Spirit / and when their meetings were betrayed by spies / and the enemies marched upon them / to ambush them / they were warned once again / when and in what place a battle would occur / and who would remain [fallen] therein / they always knew in advance / according to which they then prepared themselves / nothing happened to them / nor did they undertake anything / that the inspiration had not indicated to them beforehand. An English Knight / named Lacyus <note>John Lacy (1664–1730), a prominent English follower of the "French Prophets" who became famous for his own ecstatic fits</note>, who is said to have an annual income of nearly 5,000 Pounds Sterling / and is moreover vigorous of mind / without being inclined toward any melancholy / likewise fell into the state of inspiration. He is a member of the Society for the Reformation of Manners <note>An English organization founded in 1691 dedicated to suppressing profanity, Sunday labor, and other perceived immoralities</note> / and watched these people / as he himself reported / for several months in succession / without receiving any

any impression upon his mind / or the like / until he finally also fell into ecstasy / and / as he himself reports / the Spirit opened his mouth. He testifies furthermore / that he never desired such a thing / on the contrary <note>original: "au contraire"</note> he prayed to **GOD** more often / to spare him from it / lest perhaps Satan might have his delusion. However, after he fell into this state / he perceived within himself an uncommon joy of the Spirit and love for **GOD** / which sufficiently assured him of the presence of **GOD’s** grace. He is said to have made a blind woman see / and cured others of incurable diseases / and furthermore has the assurance from **GOD** / that he would fully receive the gift of healing <note>original: "donum sanandi"</note>. It is also said that one of them named Clary <note>Jean Clary, a famous Camisard prophet known for the "ordeal by fire"</note> discovered 2 traitors in a certain meeting / and had also learned from the Spirit / that some of those present did not recognize this as a revelation from **GOD** / for which reason he commanded / that a fire be lit immediately / into which he would throw himself / as a testimony / that it was **GOD’s** work. Although all the people fell down / and begged **GOD** / to spare them from such signs / it nevertheless happened at his insistent urging / and a great fire had to be lit / into which he jumped / and remained for over a quarter of an hour in the midst of the flames / without the slightest harm. A woman in another assembly is said / during the proclamation of **GOD's** approaching judgments / to have wept blood.

A young person is also said to have taken a sharp knife while in **ecstasy** <term>Ecstasy: A trance-like state believed to be a sign of divine possession or inspiration</term> / to prove / that he spoke through the Spirit of **GOD** / cutting himself with all his might across the chest and stomach / yet remaining unharmed by it. In the preface, the author reports how he himself received the gift of **prophecy** <term>Prophecy: In this context, the act of speaking as a direct vessel for God's voice</term> / though he did not seek it / nor did he model himself after other ecstatic individuals / but kept his imagination free / and heartily prayed to **GOD** / to preserve him from all false paths / and excesses. Despite this, he received such ecstasies and imaginings / all of which he describes at length. Afterward, he seeks to prove—both through his own example and those of other Englishmen / partly through the conversion of many **Socinians** <term>Socinians: A rationalist Christian sect that denied the Trinity; their conversion was seen as a miracle of faith over reason</term> / and also by their sincere fear of **GOD** and because they preached no new doctrine—that all of this should be attributed neither to a corrupt imagination nor to the Devil. <meta>This page continues the discussion of the "French Prophets" (Camisards) who arrived in London in 1706, following the accounts of John Lacy’s trances and the physical signs of their inspiration.</meta>

<page-num>74</page-num>

<meta>catchword: so</meta>

something entirely distinct will reveal itself in these parts concerning this new phenomenon, which currently causes such a great rumor and blinds the reason of so many people. It seems only that a great cloud has risen, and a fire, from which the **Glory of the Lord** <note>original: "Gloire des Herren"</note>—according to the truth of the prophetic figure—shall break forth in the final days. This follows the various wondrous movements of the **Cherubic wheels and eyes** <note>A reference to the vision of the prophet Ezekiel (Ezekiel 1:15–21), often used by mystical writers to describe the complex workings of Divine Providence</note>, leading toward the renewal of the form of all things, according to their certain and determined periods of time and their fates. I have observed it here and there, and it appears to me as nothing other than a great thundercloud, which may serve for the distribution and clearing of the heavens. This, I hope and believe, will finally be the result of the crashing thunderclaps that are heard almost every day from these **Camisard Prophets** <term>Camisards: French Huguenot insurgents from the Cévennes who claimed prophetic inspiration; many fled to London in the early 1700s</term> in and around this city. Within a few days, over the course of more than a year, they have made quite remarkable progress and have alarmed the entire nation in such a way that I believe nothing of the kind has ever happened before. It is indeed true, and not without merit, that in the previous **century** <note>original: "Seculo"</note> an unfolding cloud appeared within the fire itself, coming out of the North; however, that appearance is not in the least to be compared with this present one, which, in view of many causes and circumstances, is a very singular phenomenon and one of the greatest **signs of the times**. Because we therefore see these things,

<column-break/>

<page-num>75</page-num>
<header>*(0)*</header>
<meta>catchword: ihr</meta>

so let us **gird our loins** and **trim our lamps** <note>Biblical metaphors from Luke 12:35 and the Parable of the Ten Virgins, urging readiness for the Second Coming of Christ</note>, but not go out until the voice of the Shepherd of the sheep himself is recognizable and heard as distinct from others. This voice will give a clear and certain sound, so that we neither lose our crowns in the wilderness nor—as has happened to some—be taken captive in their chambers; for they indeed have a **zeal for GOD**, but one which, I fear, is not according to the truth—at least not according to the unmixed purity of the truth. I do not doubt you will have heard of very great and astonishing **failures** <note>The author likely refers to unfulfilled prophecies or public scandals involving the group</note> committed by them, which are described explicitly and in detail; yet it is certainly unprovable, lacking all appearance of truth, and indeed altogether impossible, that any secret collusion should lie beneath it. And nothing can be clearer or more manifest than that one or another kind of spirit works within them, using the instrumental powers of men, women, and children, and forcing them to speak that which—as often appears from all manifest signs—is a violent and heavy burden to them. Those who were seized in **ecstasy** <note>original: "Entzückung"</note>, as the previous letter mentioned, were from the French: **Jean Chevalier** <note>original: "Johan Mevalier"</note> and **Durand Fage** <note>original: "Duvant-Fage"</note>; and from the English: **John Lacy**, a nobleman named **John Potter**, along with two or three female persons. **A. §. 21. Quite recently, something remarkable occurred in the years 1706 and 1707 in **Zerbst** <note>A city in Saxony-Anhalt, Germany</note> with a boy of ten years named **Christlieb Leberecht von Exter**. He was the dearly beloved son of the Honorable **Johann Eberhard von Exter**, Doctor of Medicine and Personal Physician to His Royal Majesty in Prussia as well as to His Serene Highness of Anhalt. I knew this boy very well and can give testimony to him in all truth: that he was a truly God-fearing boy and a child of a good nature. This was also confirmed with a good testimony by a high Princely personage—who is a true model among the great of this world of how Princes should have Princely thoughts—and it is worthy of being made public in a dedicated treatise by **Professor Francke** <note>August Hermann Francke (1663–1727), a central figure in the Pietist movement and founder of the Francke Foundations in Halle</note>, the Professor of Holy Scripture from **Halle**. The Spirit of Jesus Christ, which comes from the Gospel, the Spirit of grace and of prayer, was so richly poured out upon him that his gestures were full of humility, and his words were full of power, faith, and childlike confidence. Often he would fetch his sister and brother to a locked chamber for prayer with these words: *Come, we have time and space to pray; we must **redeem the time**.* <note>A reference to Ephesians 5:16, meaning to make the most of every opportunity for spiritual purposes</note> To his lady mother, he once said full of joy: "Now, dear lady mother,

Nor are they yet preoccupied with **prejudices, limitations, and philosophical reasoning** <note>original: "Præjudiciis, Limitationen und Philosophischen Vernunft-Schlüssen"</note>—with which adults and the highly learned often tend to resist the Spirit of GOD, even placing their own reasoning against GOD's holy Word. This has provided a good opportunity for many older people, who had become sluggish in prayer, to recognize their own sloth.

§. 22. I can assure those who object that the prayer of the children in **Silesia** <note>A historical region in Central Europe, mostly in modern-day Poland.</note> has made a greater impression and provided more insight into the hearts of those abroad—who saw the finger of GOD in it—than it did for the locals. This is just as the **Wise Men from the East** <note>The Magi from the biblical Nativity story.</note>, as strangers enlightened by the Spirit of GOD, knew more about the newborn King of the Jews than the domestic 

<column-break/>

<page-num>81</page-num>

domestic Jews themselves, among whom He was born, and who should have been more obligated to leave a good testimony of it for posterity. Yet there were still some—whom I have partly spoken with in person and partly read—who judged the matter very well. I communicate their few testimonies here because they are born of the truth; I value them much higher than the many others whose contradictions miss the truth. One extract of a letter from **Silesia** reads as follows:

"Here in our Silesia, especially in **Lower Silesia**, most children are afoot and honor our GOD with the deepest devotion. In the district of **Liegnitz** <note>Now Legnica, Poland.</note> there is almost no village left where they do not gather daily in the quietest, most secluded manner to hold prayer hours; this also happens in the mountains and elsewhere here and there. Some of the **clergy** <note>original: "Clerisey"</note> still watch and wait; others rage horribly against it; very few rejoice over it. In Liegnitz, there are over 300 of them; they gather at 7 in the morning, 12 at noon, and 4 in the evening. They sing three hymns, pray a psalm, and read a chapter from the **Bible**. The devotion they show therein is quite extraordinary; not one of them will look up. A certain man—who said it was **Pietistic, Quaker-like, or even Heathen** <note>These were common insults used by the religious establishment to dismiss spontaneous or emotional spiritual movements.</note>—has managed to ensure they are no longer allowed out of the city; so they now pray on the...

<meta>This page continues the eyewitness accounts of the "Silesian praying children" movement (c. 1707–1708), documenting the extraordinary persistence of the children and the emotional impact their public prayer meetings had on skeptical adults and authorities.</meta>

...the circle. Many have already been severely punished by their parents with beatings; however, they will not be deterred. Locking them up also does not help, as they have jumped out of windows and broken through the stoves <note>This likely refers to tiled stoves (Kachelofen) which were often built into walls and could potentially provide a crawlspace or exit if dismantled.</note>; thus, neither the threats of the authorities, the harshest punishments of the parents, the fiercest condemnations of the **clergy** <note>original: "Clerisey"</note>, nor even forcible imprisonment has deterred them from this devout offering of prayer. Truly, GOD has something extraordinary in mind; it seems as though "at evening time it shall be light." <note>A reference to Zechariah 14:7, often interpreted as a prophecy of spiritual renewal in dark times.</note>

### Extract from another [account].

When 15 to 16 **gentlemen-at-arms** <term>Cavalliers: noblemen or mounted soldiers</term> rode toward them, it did not disturb them in their devotion at all; not even one of them looked up. I have spoken with many high-ranking officers and gentlemen who listened to them; they assured me that no one could witness their devotion without many tears. This also occurred in **Lignitz** <note>Modern-day Legnica, Poland.</note> and elsewhere among the coarsest peasant folk. It was also said that people fired blank shots among them, yet this could not unsettle them or keep them from their prayer, acting no differently than if no shots had been fired at all. When they are questioned regarding their gatherings, they constantly say: GOD had put it into their hearts. Two children—as reported from **Hirschberg** <note>Modern-day Jelenia Góra, Poland.</note>—when their parents had hidden their clothes 

<column-break/>

<page-num>83</page-num>
<header> (0) </header>
<sig>F 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: matter</meta>

so that they should not pray with the other children, wanted to jump out the windows, saying they could surely pray in their shirts. There was also a certain boy whose father had locked him in with others; he wanted to jump out the window and said to the other children that GOD would surely send His angel to protect him. When the father heard this, he opened the door and let the children go to prayer. Another child became quite ill when forbidden from going to the prayer hour, from which one could see that there was no **whim** <note>original: "Capritz"</note> or pretense among them, but rather that their whole soul was directed and driven toward it. Furthermore, the authorities sent **enforcers** <term>Frohnen: menial officers or laborers pressed into service for the state</term> to threaten to beat them and to drive them apart by force; these men had come with the intention of carrying this out. However, when they saw their devotion and unanimous prayer on their knees, these otherwise hardened people lost their courage and heart. Just as the **jailer** <note>A biblical reference to the jailer in Acts 16 who was converted after witnessing the devotion of Paul and Silas.</note> became of a different mind, those who had come like wild beasts went home like gentle sheep alongside the little flock of children.

The matter may well be noted. One also sees in his opinion that he did not yet want to hand down a **definitive** <note>original: "positivement"</note> final judgment, because he remarkably adds: "as well as I am currently informed of this matter." When he also speaks of **mixed cases** <note>original: "casibus mixtis" — situations where divine, human, and perhaps even demonic influences are intertwined</note>, which occur under the sun among men, through men, and through human mediation, I understand it in the sense in which King Solomon says in his book of Ecclesiastes in the third and other chapters, that all things—**Note Well.**—which occur under the sun, but are not worked from above, belong to vanity and have their certain time in which they begin and cease again. I also do not wish to dispute with anyone whether something disorderly might not still mingle with a true work of God; and that, according to the proverb, the devil is accustomed to build a chapel where God builds a church; also that the devil has sown weeds among the good scattered **wheat** <term>Waitzen: archaic German for "Weizen" (wheat)</term> and went away, which he can masterfully do during the spiritual slumber of men. Thus, what the Apostles and apostolic men began so well and precious later fell into decay and became very corrupted through the decline of the churches and the bishops who became lukewarm. But through all this, God nonetheless keeps his **divine hand** <note>original: "Ieov", likely a shortened reference to the Tetragrammaton or "Jehovah"</note> and his hand in the game, and preserves that which he himself has worked, which—with the evil that clings to the good—does not

<column-break/>

<page-num>85</page-num>
<header>*(0)*</header>
<sig>F 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: le (the)</meta>

not mix, but continues in the pure, unblemished track; so that he who is born of God—in so far and as long as he is and remains born of God—not only does not sin, but also cannot sin, because God’s seed remains in him. If we now apply this to the **Silesian children** <note>A 1707-1708 pietistic revival where children in Silesia gathered to pray in the fields, often causing social and political controversy.</note> from what has just been said, I say that the impulse with which the children are moved by such power described above is to be regarded as nothing other than something **extraordinary** <note>original: "extraordinaires"</note>, which God has worked in their tender hearts, and in so far as it comes from them, it is good, useful, and edifying. Should it be proven, however—which has not yet happened—that great disorders appeared and manifested themselves, then they would be **mixed cases** <note>original: "casus mixti"</note>, for the sake of which one should nonetheless not reject the good, but rather retain it once the evil has been removed. In this sense, I view the words of the highly learned Mr. **Inspector** <note>Likely a church superintendent or regional ecclesiastical overseer.</note>, when he represents and expresses the matter very well through a parable: **The course of things that happen on earth is like a string of many pearls threaded together, or like a chain of many links. All these pieces, links, and joints in themselves are human, and hang together in a human way; but God’s wonderful providence and governance is the golden thread that runs through this entire string and all its parts**

<meta>This page continues the theological reflection on how God governs the world through both ordinary and extraordinary means. ...binds all together according to His will. The Devil often wants to tear this cord; however, he can do no more than God allows him. So it remains then: God governs everything in the **Realm of His Omnipotence** and in the **Realm of His Grace**, all to His honor and for our best. And that not only in common things that happen every day, but also sometimes in quite extraordinary, strange, and wonder-worthy matters that have hardly been heard of. Meanwhile, He lets humans do their part, each according to their kind: the good doing good, the wicked doing evil, children being childish, the old acting differently—all together humanly. But the Devil is the **third party who intervenes** <note>original: "tertius interveniens"</note>, the third one who mingles in here and who, as much as he is able, spoils the good that God wants to have, but promotes the evil to which humans are inclined of themselves. Whoever now intends to pass a right judgment in such things according to this must **give to each his own** <note>original: "suum cuiqve" — a legal and moral principle of justice</note>, giving God what is God’s, man what is man’s, just as one must give no less to Satan what is his due. Furthermore, it is very laudable and well-expressed by the (Titled) Lord Inspector in what follows in his opinion: "In this matter, there is first a truly wonderful and quite uncommon providence from God, who visits our Father-
